# Excalidraw Data

## Text Elements
Linked List ^j81BnYME

A linked list is a data structure of sequence of nodes, where each node contains a value and link/reference to next node in the sequence ^oFHOtFm3

// Definition of a ListNode
class ListNode {
  constructor(val = 0, next = null) {
    this.val = val;
    this.next = next;
  }
} ^4tFIXaxi

- We can visualize a linked list as a sequence of nodes connected by arrows that point to the next node in the sequence. 

- The first node in a linked list is referred to as the head, and the last node is referred to as the tail. The next field of the tail node is None.

- unless the linked list contains a cycle which indicates the end of the linked list. ^QKRN76eu

Basic Operations ^4Xd8l8Hp

1. Traversing a Linked List ^W8o3fvv2

- We can traverse a linked list using a while loop
- use a variable `current` and point it to head of linked list
- then loop until `curren === null` in JS or `None in python`    ^8NBwP6tA

function findLength(head) {
    let current = head
    let length = 0
    while ( current !== null ) {
        length += 1
        current = current.next
    }
    return length
} ^u9SzKHIi

Time Complexity: The time complexity of this algorithm is O(n) where n is the number of nodes in the linked list. The algorithm iterates through each node in the linked list once.

Space Complexity: The space complexity of this algorithm is O(1) since we only use one pointer to traverse the linked list regardless of the number of nodes in the linked list. ^uQVsWoIk

2. Deleting a Node With a Given Target ^KV4HIZ2e

function deleteNode(head, target) {
    if (head.val == target) {
        return head.next
    }
    let current = head
    let prev = null
    while ( current !== null ) {
        if (current.val == target) {
            prev.next = current.next
            break
        }
        prev = current
        current = current.next
    }
    return head
} ^6Ku1EY5J

Complexity Analysis
Time Complexity: The time complexity of this algorithm is O(n) where n is the number of nodes in the linked list. The algorithm iterates through each node in the linked list once in the worst case (when the target does not exist in the linked list).
Space Complexity: The space complexity of this algorithm is O(1) since we only use two pointers to traverse the linked list regardless of the number of nodes in the linked list. ^4GCqMbyP

Operations to Know for Interviews ^7c1P2pya

1. Fast and Slow Pointers ^ekKx2Fy7

function fastAndSlow(head) {
    let slow = head
    let fast = head
    while ( fast !== null && fast.next !== null) {
        slow = slow.next
        fast = fast.next.next
    }
    return slow
} ^a4mEWQIv

Time Complexity: The time complexity of this algorithm is O(n) where n is the number of nodes in the linked list. The algorithm terminates when the fast pointer reaches the end. Since fast advances two nodes per iteration, the loop runs approximately n/2 times, which is O(n). ^K84TO159

Space Complexity: The space complexity of this algorithm is O(1) since we only use two pointers to traverse the linked list regardless of the number of nodes in the linked list. ^yfVPC19c

The same technique can be used to detect cycles in the linked lists.
- when slow and fast pointer point to same node in slow and fast pointer
approach we can surely say there is a cycle in linked list. 
- as in the graphical example we can see that both slow and fast pointer
met at `node 5` ^WYukkMuP

2. Reversing a linked list ^h7uf8bP2

we use three pointers. 
- current points to node we are currently reversing.
- prev is last node we will reverse and also the node that current.next will point to after reversing.
- next_ is the next node we will reverse. We need a pointer to this node before we overwrite the current.next so we can continue reversing the list in the next iteration.

When the iteration completes, current will be None, and prev will be the new head of the linked list. ^X4Dhulf3

function reverse(head){
    let current = head
    let prev = null
    while ( current !== null ) {
        let next_ = current.next
        current.next = prev
        prev = current
        current = next_
    }
    return prev
} ^xG98pO21

Time Complexity: The time complexity of this algorithm is O(n) where n is the number of nodes in the linked list. The algorithm iterates through each node in the linked list once.

Space Complexity: The space complexity of this algorithm is O(1) since we only use three pointers to reverse the linked list regardless of the number of nodes in the linked list. ^9TE3LDqh

3. Merging two linked lists ^nKxUq7Im

1 ^azv262G3

4 ^xveV9BxN

6 ^VJ2Lm3ML

3 ^WfHKI9Kj

l1 ^O5cMKn8N

l2 ^rldE1QM4

function mergeLists(l1, l2) {
    if (!l1) return l2
    if (!l2) return l1
    let head
    if ( l1.val < l2.val ) {
        head = l1
        l1 = l1.next
    }else {
        head = l2
        l2 = l2.next
    }

    let current = head
    while ( l1 !== null && l2 !== null ) {
        if ( l1.val < l2.val ) {
            current.next = l1
            l1 = l1.next
        }else {
            current.next = l2
            l2 = l2.next
        }
        current = current.next
    }
    
    if ( l1 ) current.next = l1
    if ( l2 ) current.next = l2   
    
    return head
}  ^XjJYfkWz

Time Complexity: O(n + m) where n and m are the number of nodes in the two input linked lists. The algorithm iterates through each node in the two linked lists once.

Space Complexity: O(1) We only use a constant amount of space to merge the two linked lists regardless of the number of nodes. This is because we are modifying the next pointers of the input linked lists, rather than creating new nodes. ^9NIziEkJ
